$ nl -ba tools/apply_blocker_proposal.py | sed -n '1,148p'
EXIT=0
OUTPUT_BEGIN
     1	#!/usr/bin/env python3
     2	"""Apply one reviewed blocker-proposal fragment to the registry, with obligation-loss checks.
     3	
     4	Worker proposals arrive as `registry/proposals/<worker>.json`. This tool is the parent-side
     5	integration step: it verifies the proposal against the *current* registry, refuses when the
     6	proposal would silently drop an obligation the old `next_action` carried, and only then writes.
     7	It never touches status, family, evidence, history or `shared_blockers` - only the two prose
     8	fields a proposal is allowed to change.
     9	
    10	Shapes accepted for the proposal document:
    11	
    12	* a single record: ``{"dataset": ..., "blocker": ..., "observed_append": ...,
    13	  "next_action_replacement": ...}`` (``next_action`` is accepted as an alias), or
    14	* a clause-level list: ``{"changes": [{"dataset":..., "blocker":..., "class":...,
    15	  "quoted_clause":..., "replacement":...}, ...]}`` as produced by a staleness audit. Entries for
    16	  the requested dataset/blocker are collapsed, and every entry must carry the *same*
    17	  replacement - otherwise the tool refuses rather than letting one entry overwrite another.
    18	
    19	Usage::
    20	
    21	    python3 tools/apply_blocker_proposal.py registry/proposals/w-x.json \
    22	        --dataset fever --blocker dataset-journal-gap            # dry run, reports only
    23	    python3 tools/apply_blocker_proposal.py registry/proposals/w-x.json \
    24	        --dataset fever --blocker dataset-journal-gap --apply    # writes, then re-checks
    25	
    26	Exit codes: 0 applied or clean dry run, 1 refusal or validation failure, 2 usage error.
    27	"""
    28	from __future__ import annotations
    29	
    30	import argparse
    31	import json
    32	import re
    33	import subprocess
    34	import sys
    35	from pathlib import Path
    36	
    37	ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parents[1]
    38	DEFAULT_REGISTRY = ROOT / "registry" / "dataset-blockers.json"
    39	CHECKER = ROOT / "tools" / "check_dataset_blockers.py"
    40	
    41	# Sentence boundaries used by the registry's prose. Abbreviations that must not split.
    42	_ABBREVIATIONS = ("e.g.", "i.e.", "cf.", "vs.", "etc.", "no.", "fig.", "al.")
    43	_SENTENCE_SPLIT = re.compile(r"(?<=[.;])\s+")
    44	
    45	
    46	class Refusal(ValueError):
    47	    """A proposal that must not be applied without an explicit override."""
    48	
    49	
    50	def load(path: Path) -> dict:
    51	    return json.loads(path.read_text(encoding="utf-8"))
    52	
    53	
    54	def find_blocker(document: dict, dataset: str, blocker_id: str) -> dict:
    55	    for row in document["datasets"]:
    56	        if row["dataset"] != dataset:
    57	            continue
    58	        for blocker in row["blockers"]:
    59	            if blocker["id"] == blocker_id:
    60	                return blocker
    61	    raise Refusal(f"no open blocker {dataset}/{blocker_id} in the registry")
    62	
    63	
    64	def sentences(text: str) -> list[str]:
    65	    protected = text
    66	    for index, abbreviation in enumerate(_ABBREVIATIONS):
    67	        protected = protected.replace(abbreviation, f"\x00{index}\x00")
    68	    parts = [part.strip() for part in _SENTENCE_SPLIT.split(protected) if part.strip()]
    69	    restored = []
    70	    for part in parts:
    71	        for index, abbreviation in enumerate(_ABBREVIATIONS):
    72	            part = part.replace(f"\x00{index}\x00", abbreviation)
    73	        restored.append(part)
    74	    return restored
    75	
    76	
    77	def tokens(text: str) -> set[str]:
    78	    return {token for token in re.split(r"[^0-9a-z]+", text.lower()) if token}
    79	
    80	
    81	def coverage(old_sentence: str, new_sentences: list[str]) -> float:
    82	    """Best token-containment of one old sentence in any single new sentence."""
    83	    old = tokens(old_sentence)
    84	    if not old:
    85	        return 1.0
    86	    best = 0.0
    87	    for new in new_sentences:
    88	        hit = len(old & tokens(new)) / len(old)
    89	        best = max(best, hit)
    90	    return best
    91	
    92	
    93	def collapse(proposal: dict, dataset: str, blocker: str) -> dict:
    94	    """Reduce either proposal shape to {observed_append, next_action, quoted_clauses}."""
    95	    if isinstance(proposal, list):
    96	        # A bare list of per-dataset records, as w-metriccmp emits: normalise it to the
    97	        # `changes` shape, accepting either `replacement` or `next_action_replacement`.
    98	        proposal = {"changes": [
    99	            {"dataset": item.get("dataset"), "blocker": item.get("blocker"),
   100	             "quoted_clause": item.get("quoted_clause"),
   101	             "replacement": item.get("replacement") or item.get("next_action_replacement"),
   102	             "observed_append": item.get("observed_append")}
   103	            for item in proposal]}
   104	    if "changes" in proposal:
   105	        entries = [item for item in proposal["changes"]
   106	                   if item.get("dataset") == dataset and item.get("blocker") == blocker]
   107	        if not entries:
   108	            raise Refusal(f"proposal has no change entry for {dataset}/{blocker}")
   109	        replacements = {item["replacement"] for item in entries}
   110	        if len(replacements) != 1:
   111	            raise Refusal(
   112	                f"{len(replacements)} distinct replacement strings for {dataset}/{blocker}: "
   113	                "refusing so one entry cannot overwrite another")
   114	        observed = {item.get("observed_append") or "" for item in entries}
   115	        if len(observed) != 1:
   116	            raise Refusal(
   117	                f"{len(observed)} distinct observed_append strings for {dataset}/{blocker}")
   118	        return {
   119	            "observed_append": observed.pop(), "next_action": replacements.pop(),
   120	            "quoted_clauses": [item["quoted_clause"] for item in entries if item.get("quoted_clause")],
   121	        }
   122	    next_action = proposal.get("next_action_replacement") or proposal.get("next_action")
   123	    if not next_action:
   124	        raise Refusal("proposal carries no next_action_replacement")
   125	    quoted = proposal.get("quoted_clause") or []
   126	    if isinstance(quoted, str):
   127	        quoted = [quoted]
   128	    return {"observed_append": proposal.get("observed_append") or "",
   129	            "next_action": next_action, "quoted_clauses": quoted}
   130	
   131	
   132	def normalise(text: str) -> str:
   133	    return " ".join(text.lower().split())
   134	
   135	
   136	def acknowledged(sentence: str, acknowledgements: list[str]) -> bool:
   137	    """An uncovered sentence is acknowledged when a supplied phrase matches it either way."""
   138	    target = normalise(sentence)
   139	    for phrase in acknowledgements:
   140	        if not phrase.strip():
   141	            continue
   142	        needle = normalise(phrase)
   143	        if needle in target or target in needle:
   144	            return True
   145	    return False
   146	
   147	
   148	def validate(blocker: dict, payload: dict, min_coverage: float, force: bool,
OUTPUT_END

OUTPUT_BEGIN
     1	# Metric disclosure table — w-evidence
     2	
     3	2026-10-16; source-level identity, not measured benchmark comparability. Every row refers to the snapshot and qualifications in `registry/w-evidence-qualification.md:1`. “Aligned” applies only to the stated formula/channel, not to an executed leaderboard recipe. Official-source claims inherited from the parent review are explicitly attributed; UNKNOWN is not equivalence. Historical numbers retain their original scorer, split and instrument. “Retained parent plan” means `registry/w-evidence-external-sources.md:1088`; paper-protocol detail is inherited from the retrieved handoff `/mnt/20t/xubuqiang/Study/HarnessGrad/docs/paper-fidelity-audit-handoff.md:103`, `:222`, `:263`, `:309`, not new paper/scorer execution.
     4	
     5	**One platform choice, reported six times in the intern/handoff:** `--trials N` averages a task’s measured trial scores, then the declared run policy averages measured task scores (`eval/run/runner.py:1350-1375`; `data/aggregation_policies.json:2-32`; `eval/run/metrics.py:43-44`). It does not select a verifier-best sample for test@N, estimate pass@k for k>1, or require k successes for pass^k. For n sampled attempts with c successes, mean c/n estimates pass@1; success-at-least-once and all-k-success are different statistics. At k=1 some binary definitions coincide, but protocol/split/budget still matter. The handoff explicitly maps the six repeats to GSM8K, MuSiQue, 2Wiki, τ-bench, Reasoning Gym and BIRD (`registry/w-evidence-external-sources.md:1317`). The mapping is inherited from that retrieved audit; it does not establish that every named paper uses the same pass statistic (Reasoning Gym’s aggregate remains unconfirmed). It is one aggregation decision, not six independent scorer bugs. GSM8K documents the test@N difference (`data/datasets/gsm8k.py:37-38`); LiveCodeBench explicitly limits each check to one generation (`data/datasets/livecodebench/verify/check.py:28-30`).    121	
   122	Full dispatch edges (including each handler) are explicitly enumerated in the source-edge packet; the per-requirement table below cites each external root edge. Multi-turn dynamic imports and expression-evaluation call sites remain branch-specific unresolved behavior, recorded verbatim in `source-edges.jsonl`. No expression was evaluated by this worker.
   123	
   124	Third-party recursion is represented in two distinct ways: `metadata-edges.jsonl` gives each Requires-Dist header with original metadata file/line, marker and requested extras; `installed-edges-*.jsonl` gives imports read from actually observed Python files plus the unexecuted transformers/wandb entry files. Each grouped edge has its original source path/line. `module-resolution-index.jsonl` lists the read module-to-path map; `module-frontier-*.jsonl` explicitly lists unread targets and dynamic calls as UNKNOWN. This is a bounded graph with an exact frontier, not a complete transitive runtime claim. From-import symbol names are preserved and are not assumed to be child modules. The traversal/marker rules are executable in `collect.py` and `summarize.py`.

   132	- `sys.implementation`: `{"_multiarch": "x86_64-linux-gnu", "cache_tag": "cpython-312", "hexversion": 51119344, "name": "cpython", "version": [3, 12, 4, "final", 0]}`.
   133	- Platform `linux-x86_64`; SOABI `cpython-312-x86_64-linux-gnu`; extension suffix `.cpython-312-x86_64-linux-gnu.so`.
   134	- Full compiler/configuration strings are retained in the cited record. These identify the executing interpreter, not the interpreter that built every installed extension.

   141	- Platform `linux-x86_64`; SOABI `cpython-311-x86_64-linux-gnu`; extension suffix `.cpython-311-x86_64-linux-gnu.so`.
   142	- Full compiler/configuration strings are retained in the cited record. These identify the executing interpreter, not the interpreter that built every installed extension.
   143	
   144	ELF objects are SHA-256 bound in `elf-*.jsonl`, with exact `LC_ALL=C readelf -h -d -p .comment PATH` output and the version-needs excerpt from `readelf -V PATH`. `native-links.jsonl` records each DT_NEEDED edge and same-process mapped SONAME/name candidates. A unique mapped candidate is not proof of an individual relocation binding. `/proc/self/maps` observations appear at the end of each smoke record. Exact compiled-member build Python patch version, full toolchain and reproducible build provenance remain UNKNOWN; wheel tags/ELF architecture/.comment do not prove them.
   145	
   146	Conda metadata path membership can be stale after pip upgrades. `native-owners.jsonl` separately records local archive hashes and streaming comparisons of actual archive members against installed native bytes. A false `matches` value is an observed mismatch, not custody. A true value binds those exact bytes; prefix-patched files may differ and require their own documented transformation. These comparisons do not reconstruct an environment.

   216	### 5. Denominator and multiple-alias aggregation
   217	
   218	**FOUND — official algorithm.** `evaluate_triviaqa` defaults `qid_list` to
   219	`ground_truth.keys()`; its denominator is `len(qid_list)`, not number of prediction
   220	entries, raw export rows, or aliases. Each key contributes max EM over the alias set
   221	and independently max F1; totals are multiplied by 100 and divided by this denominator
   222	(`tests/fixtures/triviaqa_upstream/evaluation/triviaqa_evaluation.py:53`, `:104`,
   223	`:120`, `:129`). Missing predictions remain in the requested-key denominator and
   224	contribute zero (`:107`). An explicitly supplied qid_list determines its own length;
   225	the function does not deduplicate that list (`:106`). The default key dictionary
   226	collapses repeated original keys by overwrite, and verified filtering happens before
   227	that construction (`tests/fixtures/triviaqa_upstream/utils/dataset_utils.py:9`, `:22`, `:40`).
   228	Human answers add normalized alternatives, not extra denominator terms
   229	(`tests/fixtures/triviaqa_upstream/evaluation/triviaqa_evaluation.py:77`). Empty ground-truth lists make the official
   230	`max` fail; local alias max returns zero and the local gold builder refuses an empty
   231	accepted set (`tests/fixtures/triviaqa_upstream/evaluation/triviaqa_evaluation.py:58`, `data/datasets/triviaqa.py:155`, `:262`).
   232	
   233	**FOUND — local metric limit.** v2 returns per-task 0/1 reward based on max normalized
   234	EM across every entry of `gold.accepted`, without an alias cap in the comparison
   235	(`data/datasets/triviaqa.py:157`, `:162`, `:269`). Its `accepted[:20]` is output
   236	diagnostic text only (`:171`). This source has no corpus-level denominator or F1
   237	aggregation; a run aggregation would require a measured task/trial receipt outside
   238	this review. The declared rc.nocontext validation count is 17,944

    42	4. **Discover the service surface.** The executable starting point is `print(apis.api_docs.show_app_descriptions())`, followed by `show_api_descriptions(app_name=...)` and `show_api_doc(app_name=..., api_name=...)`; calls use `apis.<app>.<api>(...)`. The prompt instructs discovery of supervisor APIs for credentials and use of returned/documented values (`data/datasets/appworld.py:209`, `:225–232`). The wrapper additionally exposes initialized `/api_docs`, returning `world.task.api_docs.copy()` (`data/datasets/appworld/service.py:65–69`). Its enumerated handlers are health, initialize, execute and api_docs (`:32`, `:37`, `:59`, `:65`); the wrapper's stated purpose is to avoid exposing upstream evaluation/state/log-management endpoints (`:3–6`). No deployed boundary proof is inferred from this source review.
    43	5. **Change and observe state through APIs.** “Perform the requested changes through these API actions, inspect their results”; “Print the responses you need to inspect” (`data/datasets/appworld.py:221–222`, `:234–237`). Use discovered read APIs to read back the changed state inside subsequent remote actions; the observable surface is the returned/printed API response, not an actor-written database or receipt (`data/datasets/appworld/cli/aw:53–55`; `data/datasets/appworld/service.py:62`). The service recording path is tied to `/appworld/experiments` by `ln -s /recordings/appworld/experiments /appworld/experiments` (`data/datasets/appworld/server.sh:26–29`). Platform mounts give the harness no recording and the verifier a read-only recording (`eval/service/services.py:489–496`); the prompt explicitly says it “is not an actor file to create or edit” (`data/datasets/appworld.py:236–237`).
    44	6. **Finish at both boundaries when the discovered world API requires completion.** The existing prompt's instruction is only “then give your final answer through your harness” (`data/datasets/appworld.py:234–235`). In the generic loop, a parsed answer with no command records `submitted=True`, extracts the answer and breaks; afterward `(workdir / "answer.txt").write_text(answer)` (`base_harness/loop/agent.py:668–682`, `:747`). This ends actor execution; it does not call an AppWorld API. Any in-world completion/answer must already have been submitted through the discovered API surface if it is to be represented in the replay log. W1/W2 discovery advertises `complete_task`, yet their logs omit it (W1/trace:17; W2/trace:17; both log scopes enumerated in section 3). This statement explains the generic boundary; it supplies no selected-task answer, argument values or corrected action.
    45	7. **Score the recorded world, separately from the final text.** The adapter declares a command verifier with `--log /recordings/appworld/experiments/outputs/harness/tasks/<task_id>/logs/api_calls.jsonl`, reward/evidence files, a separate verifier image and a 900-second timeout (`data/datasets/appworld.py:267–289`). The platform launches a fresh verifier with recording mounts and the task service network (`eval/verification/verifier.py:641–668`). The checker reads JSONL, creates a fresh AppWorld, replays each request, then executes `tracker = world.evaluate()` (`data/datasets/appworld/verify/check.py:31–56`). The identity is `"metric": "appworld.task_goal_completion", "metric_name": "TGC"` (`:29–30`); measured evidence contains Boolean `tracker.success`, test count, passes and failures, with reward `"1.0\n" if tracker.success else "0.0\n"` (`:61–75`). Its parser accepts task/split/log/reward/evidence arguments, **no final-answer argument** (`:23–27`). Missing/unreadable log, unknown HTTP method or environment exception writes `measured=False` and exits 3, rather than declaring a task loss (`:12–18`, `:32–37`, `:50–51`, `:59–60`).    108	
   109	A review of source and retained artifacts is **not a successful measured actor task**. All four examined runs retain losses or staged unmeasured outcomes; none establishes authentic successful actor-task evidence (section 2 artifacts; `registry/dataset-blockers.json:63`). Only a genuine successful measured actor task under the staged pilot policy can satisfy `successful-task-evidence`; the policy starts with one task/one trial after dataset gates pass and requires separate valid-receipt review before expansion (`AGENTS.md:61–65`). Source review, API activity and fabricated controls cannot substitute for it (`registry/dataset-blockers.json:63`). This review grants no new execution authorization.

    59	## Staged pilot policy
    60	
    61	- First run a one-task/one-trial diagnostic after all dataset gates pass.
    62	- For temporary reruns of blocked/incomplete datasets, select `min(30, ceil(10% * eligible))` tasks before execution, with `trials=1`, `jobs=1`.
    63	- Expand only after a separate review of a valid measured diagnostic receipt. Do not silently revert to the historical `min(50, ceil(10% * eligible)) x 3` protocol.
    64	- Invalid, unmeasured, timeout, scorer, provider, cleanup or snapshot failures are not ordinary zero scores.
    65	- Preserve old and incomplete receipts; never resume or overwrite an old run ID.
    66	
    67	## Dataset taxonomy
    68	
    69	Task capability is a cross-table: `demand x surface`. Harness coverage is a separate axis: `exercises`. Do not collapse these into one flat dataset category.

4638:## 2026-10-15 — The AppWorld fabricated-control set is complete, and two tautologies were removed from it - parent
4792:- **Correction, and it overturns two earlier statements in this journal and in the registry.** The record said the authorized appworld diagnostic *"recorded no supervisor completion request"* and that a correct task can still score zero *"when its in-world completion is omitted"*. Neither holds for that diagnostic. Its own API log has **15** calls whose last is `POST /supervisor/message` with `{"answer": ...}` — and that endpoint **is** `complete_task` (`appworld/apps/supervisor/apis.py:45-46` sets `active_task.answer = json.dumps(answer)` and `active_task.status = status`). So the completion was performed, recorded and replayed. The `<<not_given>>` observation belongs to **W1** (commit `fc27c44a`), a *different* earlier run of the same task whose log has only **14** calls and ends at a read-back with no completion — the trace hashes differ (`5d85300d…` diagnostic vs `702f60b4…` W1). The guidance gap was real before `a861ca67` and **is closed by it**: the completion call appears in the diagnostic and not in W1.
4793:- **What the diagnostic's zero actually is.** Re-running the platform's **own** checker on that frozen log, with `reward.txt`/`evidence.json` written outside the frozen run, reproduces `reward 0.0`, `success False, num_tests 8, passes 6, failures 2`: `assert answers match.` fails because the actor supplied a prose sentence where this state-change task's expected answer is `None` (`'marked "…" as not done in my bucket list note.' == 'null'`, and AppWorld's own API doc says a non-question task's answer "must be left to the default value, i.e. None"), and `assert the updated note's content rest of the lines …` fails on the note content. **So there is no platform defect and no replay defect** — the measurement path reproduces the failure faithfully, and the remaining gap is actor behaviour. Two intermediate explanations I gave were wrong and are withdrawn with it: the "decoy endpoint" reading (`/supervisor/message` *is* the completion) and the "replay loses the write" reading (it does not; `tracker.success` is false on content).   4644	## 2026-10-15 — Infrastructure failure was being published as a measured zero - w-spreadrt found it, parent fixed it
  4645	
  4646	- **How it surfaced.** `w-spreadrt` was doing the SpreadsheetBench offline-runtime work and, while fault-injecting a reward-transport loss, found that a successful real checker execution whose reward file was lost produced a host verdict of `score: 0.0`. It reported `infrastructure_failure_as_zero: "yes"` rather than filing it under "remaining gaps" as a curiosity, which is why it got fixed.
  4647	- **The parent reproduced it first-hand before touching the code.** In an isolated tree, a check that writes `verify/evidence.json` with `{"measured": true, "score": 1.0}`, prints `reward 1`, exits 0 and writes no reward file produced `score: 0.0`, `passed: False`, **no `unmeasured`**, and a verdict whose own bound evidence still carried `score: 1.0`. A published zero for a task that *was* measured successfully, in a verdict that contradicts its own evidence.
  4648	- **The code even claimed the property it violated.** `eval/verification/verifier.py`'s branch comment said it drew "the same distinction the container path draws"; the container path actually returns a verdict with **no score key at all** when `cp_out` fails, and `runner.py:1120-1131` states the rule plainly - "A check that never ran is not a zero ... recording 0.0 would blame the harness for a task nobody measured." The branch now returns `score: None` with `outcome: "unmeasured"` and a reason, keeping the check's own more specific error when it says it could not measure the task.
  4649	- **The pin required the defect, and that is worth stating plainly.** `test_exec_verdict_missing_new_reward_is_zero` asserted `score == 0.0`; it is renamed `..._is_unmeasured_not_zero` and asserts the corrected behaviour. Three security-property tests (`..._ignores_precreated_reward_and_evidence`, `..._does_not_follow_subject_symlink`, `..._clears_precreated_files_and_rejects_symlink_parent`) had asserted `0.0` *incidentally*, to show a planted `reward.txt` containing `1.0` was not adopted; their real property is "the planted value is not adopted", which now shows as no published score plus an unmeasured outcome. Each was rewritten to assert that directly, with a comment, and each still checks the planted file is untouched or cleared. No assertion was deleted, and a new regression test pins the reproduced case.
  4650	- **Zero new failures, checked against `main` rather than assumed.** `test_verifier.py` 23 passed. The broader verdict-related set is 170 passed / 9 skipped / 2 failed, and both failures were verified independently: `test_snapshot_covers_the_referee_not_the_candidate` needs the gitignored `.env` and **passes on `main`**, while `test_the_harness_cannot_read_the_platform_or_its_answers` **fails on `main` too**. Both are now recorded in `registry/known-red-tests.md`, the first in a new "worktree-only failures" section, so a worktree run is not misread as a regression.
  4651	- **Separate and still open:** the same worker's synthetic stale-cache control FAILED - a real LibreOffice 7.3.7.2 conversion retained authored stale caches `[999,888]` instead of `[5,10]` and the local checker still published `recalculated=true, measured=true, score=0.0`. That is a different question from this reward-transport defect and is recorded in its proposal, not fixed here. Checker valid; merged as 1ac69e91.

  4788	- **Resource state recorded rather than manufactured work (objective clause 4).** At `2026-10-15T16:42Z` both A100s report **0 % utilization**; GPU0 carries our `http://127.0.0.1:8002/v1` endpoint's resident weights (36,138 MiB, an 11-hour-old `VLLM::EngineCore` under `xubuqiang`), GPU1 carries a second long-running engine (36,138 MiB, 2 days) plus **another user's** 700 MiB process (`zhuanbang`), and the endpoint answers `200` on `/v1/models`.   4790	## 2026-10-15 (late) — The appworld "invisible answer" was two different runs, and a reachability result for BFCL
  4791	
  4792	- **Correction, and it overturns two earlier statements in this journal and in the registry.** The record said the authorized appworld diagnostic *"recorded no supervisor completion request"* and that a correct task can still score zero *"when its in-world completion is omitted"*. Neither holds for that diagnostic. Its own API log has **15** calls whose last is `POST /supervisor/message` with `{"answer": ...}` — and that endpoint **is** `complete_task` (`appworld/apps/supervisor/apis.py:45-46` sets `active_task.answer = json.dumps(answer)` and `active_task.status = status`). So the completion was performed, recorded and replayed. The `<<not_given>>` observation belongs to **W1** (commit `fc27c44a`), a *different* earlier run of the same task whose log has only **14** calls and ends at a read-back with no completion — the trace hashes differ (`5d85300d…` diagnostic vs `702f60b4…` W1). The guidance gap was real before `a861ca67` and **is closed by it**: the completion call appears in the diagnostic and not in W1.
  4793	- **What the diagnostic's zero actually is.** Re-running the platform's **own** checker on that frozen log, with `reward.txt`/`evidence.json` written outside the frozen run, reproduces `reward 0.0`, `success False, num_tests 8, passes 6, failures 2`: `assert answers match.` fails because the actor supplied a prose sentence where this state-change task's expected answer is `None` (`'marked "…" as not done in my bucket list note.' == 'null'`, and AppWorld's own API doc says a non-question task's answer "must be left to the default value, i.e. None"), and `assert the updated note's content rest of the lines …` fails on the note content. **So there is no platform defect and no replay defect** — the measurement path reproduces the failure faithfully, and the remaining gap is actor behaviour. Two intermediate explanations I gave were wrong and are withdrawn with it: the "decoy endpoint" reading (`/supervisor/message` *is* the completion) and the "replay loses the write" reading (it does not; `tracker.success` is false on content). The registry now carries the correction as an explicit superseding paragraph (`d25a71a1`).
  4794	- **Method note, because this cost three reversals.** The two runs shared a task id, a run directory name shape and an evidence vocabulary, and I compared evidence from one against logs from the other. **A task id is not a run id.** The check that settles it is cheap and should be first: hash the traces, or count the calls, on both sides before drawing any conclusion across runs.
  4795	- **`w-bfclguard` merged (`3a48f51a`, registry `8aed83a9`) — a scoped reachability result, which is the shape this blocker needs.** `FOUND`: the platform's BFCL name scorer and shared verifier completed four fabricated scoring controls without importing the retained decoder. `ABSENT over an enumerated scope`: 317 files (308 `.py` + nine shebang scripts) under `driver.py`, `eval/`, `data/`, `tools/`, `harnessgrad/`, `ckpt/`, `methods/`, each pinned by bytes and SHA-256, every one treated as an entry point, 2,115 import-graph records — `known_target_imports: 0`, `target_mechanisms: 0`, with the only target references being two data-path string constants. `UNKNOWN`: computed loaders, generated code, process argv, dependencies, and anything outside that scope. The preflight gate exists with controls but is **unwired**, and no platform call site was edited. **The pinned inventory had to be refreshed at integration** because two scoped sources changed after the worker's base (this session's launch-failure and relayed-signal fix): the documented regeneration moved `import_nodes` 2115 → 2116 — exactly the new `import signal` — and left both target counters at 0, so the conclusion does not depend on the refresh. Verified 168 passed across the reachability, containment, AST-contract, import-direction, decoding and registry guards.

s=s.replace('@@APP_GATE@@',parts['appworld'][6]).replace('@@BFCL_GATE@@',parts['bfcl_v3'][12]).replace('@@BFCL_BUILD@@',parts['bfcl_v3'][1]).replace('@@TRIVIA_GATE@@',parts['triviaqa'][0]).replace('@@TRIVIA_AUDIT@@',parts['triviaqa'][3])
p.write_text(s)
print('Wrote',p, 'bytes=',p.stat().st_size)
print('Current next_action sentence counts:',{k:len(v) for k,v in parts.items()})
PY

This is a choice sheet, not a recommendation or authorization. This states the current success gate, without re-imposing the withdrawn shared admission gates (AW8).

**Obtainability: UNKNOWN prospectively; not ruled out by the failed task.** The parent reports faithful measurement of a real failure, not a success or an impossibility theorem (`docs/adapter-integration-journal.md:4793`; `registry/dataset-blockers.json:63`). Existing source-only/control evidence cannot supply the missing observation (AW7). wg-w-decisionpack
# HarnessGrad Agent Operating Rules

## Execution priorities

1. Close automatically solvable blockers and internalize reusable platform capabilities before launching large pilots.
2. Use isolated Codex worktrees only. Parent reviews every diff, runs focused tests, rejects stale reports and unrelated changes, then merges safe commits and updates the journal.
3. Keep up to 10 independent Codex tasks active when work can be parallelized. Every task must have a unique output filename; never trust inherited `reports/report.md` without checking the diff and commit.
4. Avoid idle rounds: combine waits with concrete receipt inspection, diff review, test execution, journal work or launch preparation. Do not spend a round only polling unchanged status.

## Working copies

There are exactly **two writers**, and the rule is about who yields to whom:

- **The operator writes directly to the main checkout** `/mnt/20t/xubuqiang/Study/HarnessGrad`
  whenever they want. That is supported, not a conflict. An agent merges **on top of** whatever
  it finds there and never rewrites, rebases away, reverts or moves an operator commit. When a
  merge conflicts because the operator changed the same file, the operator's change is the
  starting point and the agent's is the one that has to fit around it.
- **Agents never commit to `main`.** They work in registered `git worktree` checkouts under
  `/tmp/hg-*`, commit there, and the parent reviews the diff and merges.
- Every *other* copy of this repository on this machine is read-only or disposable.
- **Never create a full clone of this repository inside `Study/`.** Two were made on 2026-11-17
  (`HarnessGrad-appworld-controls-20261117`, `HarnessGrad-gaia-controls-20261117`). Both are now
  stranded on pre-rewrite commits that do not exist in `main`'s object store, and both still
  carry the old layout (`web/dist`, root-level report files). Their `origin` remotes -- which
  pointed at the main checkout, fetch **and push** -- have been removed, because a single
  `git push --force` from either would have restored the old history over `main`.
- They are kept, not deleted, and not reset: they hold run receipts and evidence
  (`gaia-evidence/`, `gaia-evidence-all17/`, `GAIA_FINAL_LAUNCH_RECEIPT.json`, the appworld
  `deliverables/` and `runtime/`) that exist nowhere else, and rule 5 of the staged pilot policy
  says old and incomplete receipts are preserved. Treat them as frozen evidence: read them, never
  fetch, merge, push or "update" them.
- 10 of 20 local branches also still point at pre-rewrite commits. They are refs, not working
  copies, so they are not a writing hazard, but they keep the old history reachable; decide
  explicitly before deleting any, since they are the only record of that codex work.
- If a copy is ever needed for an isolated run, make it a `git worktree` (registered, so
  `git worktree list` shows it) rather than a clone, and remove it when the run is done.

## Test execution

Pytest `--basetemp` must be **OUTSIDE the platform tree**, for example
`--basetemp=/tmp/harnessgrad-pytest-quality`. Driver-based tests use `tmp_path`
as a harness work root, which the runner correctly refuses inside the platform.
Omitting the option preserves pytest's normal default.

## GPU execution

- **One local endpoint: `http://127.0.0.1:8002/v1`** (model `qwen3.5-9b-local`). GPU0 and the
  `8001` server were released on 2026-10-14 to give a card back; do not start a second one.
- `eval/unix_model_relay.py` hard-codes the agent model route to `127.0.0.1:8002`, so `8002` is
  the endpoint a containerised harness actually reaches. That, not preference, is why this is
  the one that was kept -- the other would have needed a production change.
- Keep the endpoint productively occupied with necessary no-model controls, diagnostics or valid
  pilots; never create duplicate, fake or scientifically unjustified work for utilization. If
  there is no approved diagnostic or pilot, an idle loaded server is the correct state: record
  that rather than inventing work.
- Bind each run to one endpoint and one fresh run ID. Keep total H0 concurrency `<=3`, `jobs=1`.

## Staged pilot policy

- First run a one-task/one-trial diagnostic after all dataset gates pass.
- For temporary reruns of blocked/incomplete datasets, select `min(30, ceil(10% * eligible))` tasks before execution, with `trials=1`, `jobs=1`.
- Expand only after a separate review of a valid measured diagnostic receipt. Do not silently revert to the historical `min(50, ceil(10% * eligible)) x 3` protocol.
- Invalid, unmeasured, timeout, scorer, provider, cleanup or snapshot failures are not ordinary zero scores.
- Preserve old and incomplete receipts; never resume or overwrite an old run ID.

## Dataset taxonomy

Task capability is a cross-table: `demand x surface`. Harness coverage is a separate axis: `exercises`. Do not collapse these into one flat dataset category.

## Prohibited shortcuts

No method tuning, paid endpoints, heldout leakage, SOTUQA, API-Bank, NESTFUL, Tau2-bench, Matrix, score fabrication or fake benchmark utilization.

## Evidence requirements

Every diagnostic or pilot records metric identity, eligible/selected/task/trial coverage, invalid and unmeasured counts, source/split/manifest/model/harness hashes, endpoint binding, resource/budget limits, cleanup receipt and comparability notes.

## Blocker closure priorities

Prioritize selected-only/privacy and provenance, scorer dependency/network and offline closure, trusted verifier/runtime, artifact-only state export, filesystem quota enforcement, official scorer execution, snapshot lifecycle and cleanup. A passing synthetic control is not a production H0 authorization.
OUTPUT_END

registry/w-evidence-qualification.md:428:## Handoff corrections retained with the qualifications
     1	#!/usr/bin/env python3
     2	"""Apply one reviewed blocker-proposal fragment to the registry, with obligation-loss checks.
     3	
     4	Worker proposals arrive as `registry/proposals/<worker>.json`. This tool is the parent-side
     5	integration step: it verifies the proposal against the *current* registry, refuses when the
     6	proposal would silently drop an obligation the old `next_action` carried, and only then writes.
     7	It never touches status, family, evidence, history or `shared_blockers` - only the two prose
     8	fields a proposal is allowed to change.
     9	
    10	Shapes accepted for the proposal document:
    11	
    12	* a single record: ``{"dataset": ..., "blocker": ..., "observed_append": ...,
    13	  "next_action_replacement": ...}`` (``next_action`` is accepted as an alias), or
    14	* a clause-level list: ``{"changes": [{"dataset":..., "blocker":..., "class":...,
    15	  "quoted_clause":..., "replacement":...}, ...]}`` as produced by a staleness audit. Entries for
    16	  the requested dataset/blocker are collapsed, and every entry must carry the *same*
    17	  replacement - otherwise the tool refuses rather than letting one entry overwrite another.
    18	
    19	Usage::
    20	
    21	    python3 tools/apply_blocker_proposal.py registry/proposals/w-x.json \
    22	        --dataset fever --blocker dataset-journal-gap            # dry run, reports only
    23	    python3 tools/apply_blocker_proposal.py registry/proposals/w-x.json \
    24	        --dataset fever --blocker dataset-journal-gap --apply    # writes, then re-checks
    25	
    26	Exit codes: 0 applied or clean dry run, 1 refusal or validation failure, 2 usage error.
    27	"""
    28	from __future__ import annotations
    29	
    30	import argparse
    31	import json
    32	import re
    33	import subprocess
    34	import sys
    35	from pathlib import Path
    36	
    37	ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parents[1]
    38	DEFAULT_REGISTRY = ROOT / "registry" / "dataset-blockers.json"
    39	CHECKER = ROOT / "tools" / "check_dataset_blockers.py"
    40	
    41	# Sentence boundaries used by the registry's prose. Abbreviations that must not split.
    42	_ABBREVIATIONS = ("e.g.", "i.e.", "cf.", "vs.", "etc.", "no.", "fig.", "al.")
    43	_SENTENCE_SPLIT = re.compile(r"(?<=[.;])\s+")
    44	
    45	
    46	class Refusal(ValueError):
    47	    """A proposal that must not be applied without an explicit override."""
    48	
    49	
    50	def load(path: Path) -> dict:
    51	    return json.loads(path.read_text(encoding="utf-8"))
    52	
    53	
    54	def find_blocker(document: dict, dataset: str, blocker_id: str) -> dict:
    55	    for row in document["datasets"]:
    56	        if row["dataset"] != dataset:
    57	            continue
    58	        for blocker in row["blockers"]:
    59	            if blocker["id"] == blocker_id:
    60	                return blocker
    61	    raise Refusal(f"no open blocker {dataset}/{blocker_id} in the registry")
    62	
    63	
    64	def sentences(text: str) -> list[str]:
    65	    protected = text
    66	    for index, abbreviation in enumerate(_ABBREVIATIONS):
    67	        protected = protected.replace(abbreviation, f"\x00{index}\x00")
    68	    parts = [part.strip() for part in _SENTENCE_SPLIT.split(protected) if part.strip()]
    69	    restored = []
    70	    for part in parts:
    71	        for index, abbreviation in enumerate(_ABBREVIATIONS):
    72	            part = part.replace(f"\x00{index}\x00", abbreviation)
    73	        restored.append(part)
    74	    return restored
    75	
    76	
    77	def tokens(text: str) -> set[str]:
    78	    return {token for token in re.split(r"[^0-9a-z]+", text.lower()) if token}
    79	
    80	
    81	def coverage(old_sentence: str, new_sentences: list[str]) -> float:
    82	    """Best token-containment of one old sentence in any single new sentence."""
    83	    old = tokens(old_sentence)
    84	    if not old:
    85	        return 1.0
    86	    best = 0.0
    87	    for new in new_sentences:
    88	        hit = len(old & tokens(new)) / len(old)
    89	        best = max(best, hit)
    90	    return best
    91	
    92	
    93	def collapse(proposal: dict, dataset: str, blocker: str) -> dict:
    94	    """Reduce either proposal shape to {observed_append, next_action, quoted_clauses}."""
    95	    if isinstance(proposal, list):
    96	        # A bare list of per-dataset records, as w-metriccmp emits: normalise it to the
    97	        # `changes` shape, accepting either `replacement` or `next_action_replacement`.
    98	        proposal = {"changes": [
    99	            {"dataset": item.get("dataset"), "blocker": item.get("blocker"),
   100	             "quoted_clause": item.get("quoted_clause"),
   101	             "replacement": item.get("replacement") or item.get("next_action_replacement"),
   102	             "observed_append": item.get("observed_append")}
   103	            for item in proposal]}
   104	    if "changes" in proposal:
   105	        entries = [item for item in proposal["changes"]
   106	                   if item.get("dataset") == dataset and item.get("blocker") == blocker]
   107	        if not entries:
   108	            raise Refusal(f"proposal has no change entry for {dataset}/{blocker}")
   109	        replacements = {item["replacement"] for item in entries}
   110	        if len(replacements) != 1:
   111	            raise Refusal(
   112	                f"{len(replacements)} distinct replacement strings for {dataset}/{blocker}: "
   113	                "refusing so one entry cannot overwrite another")
   114	        observed = {item.get("observed_append") or "" for item in entries}
   115	        if len(observed) != 1:
   116	            raise Refusal(
   117	                f"{len(observed)} distinct observed_append strings for {dataset}/{blocker}")
   118	        return {
   119	            "observed_append": observed.pop(), "next_action": replacements.pop(),
   120	            "quoted_clauses": [item["quoted_clause"] for item in entries if item.get("quoted_clause")],
   121	        }
   122	    next_action = proposal.get("next_action_replacement") or proposal.get("next_action")
   123	    if not next_action:
   124	        raise Refusal("proposal carries no next_action_replacement")
   125	    quoted = proposal.get("quoted_clause") or []
   126	    if isinstance(quoted, str):
   127	        quoted = [quoted]
   128	    return {"observed_append": proposal.get("observed_append") or "",
   129	            "next_action": next_action, "quoted_clauses": quoted}
   130	
   131	
   132	def normalise(text: str) -> str:
   133	    return " ".join(text.lower().split())
   134	
   135	
   136	def acknowledged(sentence: str, acknowledgements: list[str]) -> bool:
   137	    """An uncovered sentence is acknowledged when a supplied phrase matches it either way."""
   138	    target = normalise(sentence)
   139	    for phrase in acknowledgements:
   140	        if not phrase.strip():
   141	            continue
   142	        needle = normalise(phrase)
   143	        if needle in target or target in needle:
   144	            return True
   145	    return False
   146	
   147	
   148	def validate(blocker: dict, payload: dict, min_coverage: float, force: bool,
   149	             acknowledgements: list[str]) -> list[str]:
   150	    """Return the refusal list; empty means the proposal may be applied."""
   151	    problems: list[str] = []
   152	    old_action = blocker["next_action"]
   153	    new_action = payload["next_action"]
   154	    for clause in payload["quoted_clauses"]:
   155	        if clause not in old_action:
   156	            problems.append(f"quoted_clause not present verbatim in next_action: {clause[:90]!r}")
   157	    if new_action.strip() == old_action.strip():
   158	        problems.append("next_action_replacement is identical to the current value")
   159	    unacknowledged = [(ratio, text) for ratio, text in _uncovered(old_action, new_action, min_coverage)
   160	                      if not acknowledged(text, acknowledgements)]
   161	    if unacknowledged and not force:
   162	        problems.append(
   163	            f"{len(unacknowledged)} sentence(s) of the current next_action are not carried by the "
   164	            "replacement and were not acknowledged; pass --acknowledge for each one you intend to "
   165	            "drop (or --force after reading them):")
   166	        problems.extend(f"    coverage {ratio:.2f}: {text}" for ratio, text in unacknowledged)
   167	    return problems
   168	
   169	
   170	def _uncovered(old_action: str, new_action: str, min_coverage: float) -> list[tuple[float, str]]:
   171	    new_sentences = sentences(new_action)
   172	    uncovered = []
   173	    for old_sentence in sentences(old_action):
   174	        ratio = coverage(old_sentence, new_sentences)
   175	        if ratio < min_coverage:
   176	            uncovered.append((ratio, old_sentence))
   177	    return uncovered
   178	
   179	
   180	def main(argv: list[str] | None = None) -> int:
   181	    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(description=__doc__,
   182	                                     formatter_class=argparse.RawDescriptionHelpFormatter)
   183	    parser.add_argument("proposal", type=Path)
   184	    parser.add_argument("--dataset", required=True)
   185	    parser.add_argument("--blocker", required=True)
   186	    parser.add_argument("--registry", type=Path, default=DEFAULT_REGISTRY)
   187	    parser.add_argument("--min-coverage", type=float, default=0.6)
   188	    parser.add_argument("--acknowledge", action="append", default=[], metavar="TEXT",
   189	                        help="a phrase naming a clause you intend to drop; repeatable")
   190	    parser.add_argument("--force", action="store_true",
   191	                        help="apply despite unacknowledged dropped sentences")
   192	    parser.add_argument("--apply", action="store_true",
   193	                        help="write the registry; without it this is a dry run")
   194	    args = parser.parse_args(argv)
   195	
   196	    document = load(args.registry)
   197	    try:
   198	        blocker = find_blocker(document, args.dataset, args.blocker)
   199	        payload = collapse(load(args.proposal), args.dataset, args.blocker)
   200	        problems = validate(blocker, payload, args.min_coverage, args.force, args.acknowledge)
   201	    except Refusal as error:
   202	        print(f"REFUSED: {error}", file=sys.stderr)
   203	        return 1
   204	    if problems:
   205	        print("REFUSED:", file=sys.stderr)
   206	        for problem in problems:
   207	            print(f"  {problem}", file=sys.stderr)
   208	        return 1
   209	
   210	    old_action, old_observed = blocker["next_action"], blocker["observed"]
   211	    forced = [(r, t) for r, t in _uncovered(old_action, payload["next_action"], args.min_coverage)
   212	              if not acknowledged(t, args.acknowledge)]
   213	    if forced:
   214	        print(f"WARNING: {len(forced)} uncovered sentence(s) applied without acknowledgement:",
   215	              file=sys.stderr)
   216	        for ratio, text in forced:
   217	            print(f"    coverage {ratio:.2f}: {text}", file=sys.stderr)
   218	    already = bool(payload["observed_append"]) and payload["observed_append"].strip() in old_observed
   219	    if payload["observed_append"] and not already:
   220	        blocker["observed"] = old_observed.rstrip() + " " + payload["observed_append"].strip()
   221	    blocker["next_action"] = payload["next_action"].strip()
   222	
   223	    print(f"{args.dataset}/{args.blocker}")
   224	    print(f"  next_action  {len(old_action)} -> {len(blocker['next_action'])} chars")
   225	    print(f"  observed     {len(old_observed)} -> {len(blocker['observed'])} chars"
   226	          + ("  (append already present: no-op)" if already else ""))
   227	    if not args.apply:
   228	        print("  dry run: nothing written (pass --apply)")
   229	        return 0
   230	
   231	    original = args.registry.read_text(encoding="utf-8")
   232	    args.registry.write_text(json.dumps(document, indent=2, ensure_ascii=False) + "\n",
   233	                             encoding="utf-8")
   234	    checked = subprocess.run([sys.executable, str(CHECKER), "--registry", str(args.registry)],
   235	                             capture_output=True, text=True, errors="replace", cwd=ROOT)
   236	    output = (checked.stdout or checked.stderr).strip()
   237	    print(f"  checker: {output}")
   238	    if checked.returncode != 0 or '"valid": true' not in output:
   239	        args.registry.write_text(original, encoding="utf-8")
   240	        print("  checker did not validate: registry restored", file=sys.stderr)
   241	        return 1
   242	    print("  applied")
   243	    return 0
   244	
   245	
   246	if __name__ == "__main__":
   247	    raise SystemExit(main())
OUTPUT_END

### 4. Observation needed for outright closure

The original standard needs producer-authenticated manifests bound to the exact retained archive/member digests, naming source commits, CPython build implementation/version/SOABI, compiler/flags and compiled inputs; exact parent/child loader/dlopen observations; complete input-bound license/NOTICE scope; a strict retained-filename websockets provenance record; a concrete handler/config/grammar and privacy-preserving full-call contract; a bounded/bypassed expression decoder with `guarded_decode` or an explicit justified alternative; and an authentic official-entrypoint execution receipt naming source/runtime/input/metric/results (`registry/dataset-blockers.json:195`; `registry/w-bfclrt-custody.md:883`). A publication receipt and a static provider list alone do not meet that standard (BF3).

**Obtainability: no complete closure from the retained evidence described by these records; upstream recoverability is UNKNOWN.** The canonical frontier still records compiled-build UNKNOWNs, and public producer records outside its enumerated URLs are explicitly unsearched (`registry/w-bfclrt-custody.md:881-883`). Runtime execution can add loader observations but cannot observe a historical compiler invocation. Producer-authenticated historical records might settle it; the cited search does not prove those records do not exist. Therefore an unconditional “unobtainable everywhere” statement would be invented. With existing evidence and no new access/execution, the choice is open indefinitely (HOLD/RECORD) or closed with the named limit (LIMIT); SEEK purchases an attempt with no promise of full closure.

### 4. Observation needed for outright closure

An authentic immutable upstream execution receipt must name export `0f7faf33a3908546c6fd5b73a660e0f8ff173c2f`, the actually executed loader/converter and source/license hashes, runtime/writer versions and consumed input revisions/hashes. A source/split-bound prose counted-ID/key inventory or authentic correction must explain 18669 versus stored 17944; the twelve-versus-eight declaration question needs no repeat (`registry/dataset-blockers.json:740`; `registry/w-trivia4-reconciliation.md:59-66`, `:102-105`, `:163-170`; `registry/w-trivia2-review.md:242-251`).

**Obtainability: the retained payload-free scopes cannot produce the missing historical execution/answer-preservation observations; recoverability outside them is UNKNOWN.** The named public search found no receipt in its accessible responses, while authenticated jobs, unsupported historical interfaces, inaccessible snapshots/index and original maintainer artifacts remained unobserved. The review explicitly rejects universal unavailability and sets withdrawal_entry to null (`registry/w-trivia2-review.md:264-297`). Those are inherited bounded search results, not a search run here; exact scopes, request plans and commands are cited in that review and its validation. A converter run today is prospective evidence only (`:294-297`). Thus existing evidence plus no new access supports open indefinitely (HOLD/RECORD) or an operator-selected named limit (LIMIT), not “no receipt can exist.” SEEK is conditional on new access, with no guarantee of closure.
